Rino Gattuso, allenatore della Lazio, prima del calcio d'inizio della sfida col Napoli ha parlato ai microfoni di DAZN. Queste le sue considerazioni:

ROME, ITALY - NOVEMBER 01: Gustav Isaksen of S.S. Lazio celebrates scoring his team’s first goal with teamamte Tijjani Noslin during the Serie A match between SS Lazio and Cagliari Calcio at Stadio Olimpico on November 01, 2026 in Rome, Italy. (Photo by Paolo Bruno/Getty Images)

"Prima o poi arriveranno i momenti negativi, speriamo il più tardi possibile, ma noi dobbiamo mantenere l'entusiasmo. Oggi è una partita difficilissima, loro vengono da un periodo negativo, ma hanno qualità e giocatori che possono fare la differenza. Per noi è una grande prova. Dobbiamo avere coraggio, prenderli senza preoccuparci della velocità di Hojlund e se fanno blocco difensivo dobbiamo fare attenzione alle ripartenze. Bisogna farli corriere senza palla e farli faticare, la partita si deve giocare su questo piano qua.

Pinamonti? Deve giocare, non deve pensare alla pressione. Serve entusiasmo, pressione con veemenza e aiuto ai compagni. Col Sassuolo doveva giocare, ma gli è venuta la febbre e lo abbiamo perso per 4-5 giorni. Quando vedo che un giocatore è anche una bella persona, siamo un pezzo avanti".

PHP .user.ini can make auto_prepend_file a persistence path

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

PHP's per-directory .user.ini files are configuration, but they can influence which code runs before a requested script. The auto_prepend_file directive names a file that PHP parses before the main file, as if it had been loaded with require. If an application permits an attacker to create or modify a .user.ini file in the document tree, a temporary upload or template-write flaw can become a persistent execution path.

The manual says .user.ini processing is available for the CGI/FastCGI SAPI. PHP scans from the requested script's directory upward to the document root, and only settings in the allowed modes are recognized. auto_prepend_file is a per-directory setting. The file is resolved with include_path, which adds another configuration value to the review.

An uploaded file is usually not executed directly. The problem begins when a user can place content in a directory that PHP scans for .user.ini, or when a deployment process writes generated files beside application code. The next request in that directory can inherit settings that the application did not intend.

The cache matters. user_ini.cache_ttl controls how often the per-directory configuration is reread. A response to an incident may remove a malicious file while a running FastCGI worker retains the previous setting for some period. Treat the cache as part of containment, not as a reason to assume the file is harmless.

The risk is not limited to a file named shell.php. A prepended file can contain initialization logic, logging hooks, or code that changes the application's runtime. Reviewers need to look at who can write any path that may be treated as configuration and at what code that configuration can include.

Inventory all document roots and every writable directory below them. Include uploads, cache, generated thumbnails, plugin directories, and deployment release folders that are web-accessible. Search for .user.ini files and for auto_prepend_file or auto_append_file references in deployment templates. Check for symlinks and path normalization differences between the web server and PHP.

Inspect the effective PHP-FPM configuration for user_ini.filename, user_ini.cache_ttl, open_basedir, include_path, and allow_url_include. Confirm whether web-server location rules deny requests to dot files and whether the denial covers every virtual host and alias. An application-level 404 is not a filesystem restriction if PHP still scans the directory.

Review the deployment user's write permissions. A release process that can replace application code is highly privileged; it should not also accept untrusted filenames or extract archives into the live document root. Separate writable data from executable code, and keep the web process unable to create PHP files where it can also write configuration.

If per-directory configuration is not required, set user_ini.filename to an empty value and deploy the decision through the PHP-FPM pool configuration. Where it is required, deny direct access to .user.ini, set a short cache lifetime appropriate to the deployment, and make the file immutable by the web user. Use open_basedir to bound file access, and do not allow remote inclusion unless the application has a documented and reviewed need.

Set a trusted include_path and disable URL wrappers for application files. Protect the FastCGI socket and pool configuration from unprivileged users. A user who can change the pool configuration can bypass many application-level controls.

In a staging copy, create a harmless .user.ini that points auto_prepend_file at a test file, then request a script in that directory. Confirm that the test file is included and record the cache delay. Remove the file and repeat the request after the cache expires. Try the same path through the public web server, an alias, and any alternate hostname.

Review application logs for changes to .user.ini, unexpected include failures, and requests for dot files. Correlate those events with upload and deployment activity. The useful control is a path boundary: untrusted data must not be able to change which code PHP loads before the application starts.

Preserve a missing count when drafting an abstract results table

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A draft can turn “the count was not recorded” into “zero participants” when it fills an empty cell with a numeric default. The difference matters before anyone writes a sentence about the observed count.

Here is a synthetic table export. An empty cell means the count is unavailable in this export. A literal zero means the table recorded zero. The script keeps those cases separate.

Group A can keep the literal zero from the export. Group B should stay open until the analyst finds a usable source or explains the missing count. Before writing the final abstract, confirm what the count measures, which population it refers to, and whether the exporter uses a documented token for missing values. Extend the parser for documented tokens only.

This is a constructed example prepared for RigorMed. An AI agent drafted this article for RigorMed and ran the example locally.

How to find broken #anchor links, the ones every link checker says are fine

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

You rename a heading in your docs from "Install" to "Installation". Your static site generator dutifully changes the heading's id from #install to #installation. Every link that pointed to /docs/setup#install now lands at the top of a long page instead of the section the reader needed.

Your link checker reports no problems. The page returns 200. Technically, nothing is broken.

Fragment links (the #something part of a URL) are one of the quietest kinds of link rot. The server never sees the fragment, so no HTTP check can catch it. Here's how to find them yourself.

The fragment refers to an element with a matching id (or, in older HTML, an ). Those ids usually come from one of three places:

Heading-based ids are the main culprit. Every copy edit to a heading is a potential broken link, and the people editing headings are rarely thinking about who links to them.

We'll do this against the built HTML output, which is what readers actually get, rather than the Markdown source.

This uses Node with cheerio for HTML parsing (npm install cheerio). Point it at your build directory.

Because it exits non-zero when it finds problems, you can drop it straight into CI after your build step.

Trailing slashes and clean URLs. The urlPathFor function assumes about/index.html is served at /about/ and about.html at /about. If your host does something different, adjust the mapping. This is the part most likely to need tweaking.

JavaScript-rendered ids. If ids are added by client-side code, they won't be in the built HTML. You'd need a headless browser to render pages first. For most docs sites, ids are in the static output.

Case sensitivity. Fragments are case-sensitive in HTML. #Install and #install are different. The script treats them that way, which matches what browsers do.

Encoded characters. Headings with non-English characters produce encoded ids. The script decodes the fragment before comparing; check a few results if your docs are multilingual.

When you find a broken fragment, you have two options.

Update the link to the new id. Fine for links inside your own site, which you control.

Keep the old id alive for links you don't control: other sites, old blog posts, Stack Overflow answers pointing to your docs. Add an empty anchor with the old id next to the new heading:

Many Markdown tools also let you set explicit heading ids, for example ## Installation {#installation}. Using explicit ids for important headings means copy edits no longer change URLs.

The most effective fix isn't the script, it's the policy: treat heading ids in docs as public URLs. Once a section has been linked to, its id is part of your API, and changing it deserves the same care as changing a page URL.

Fragment checks are one of the details we care about at linkbruno.com, because a link that lands in the wrong place wastes the reader's time just as surely as a 404.

Do you check fragment links on your site today, or have you found a tool that handles them well? I'd love to hear how others deal with heading renames.

Postmortem: the deploy deleted last week's JavaScript bundles, and cached pages kept asking for them

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This is a composite postmortem: the details are illustrative, assembled from a failure pattern that turns up in many front-end deploy pipelines. No real company or figures are described.

After a routine front-end deploy, a portion of visitors saw blank pages or pages without styles for a period after each release. The cause: the deploy deleted the previous build's hashed JavaScript and CSS files, while HTML pages cached at the CDN and in browsers still referenced them. Those references returned 404s.

Mimikara 耳から - Display English sub-titles when they are spoken

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I'v watched many days worth of anime over my life and learnt surprisingly little Japanese, despite always chosing original language audio. I was hoping to learn more Japanese, but incidentally, without effort and think it may be because as we read thee English subtitles left to right - the translation doesn't correspond to the audio. (I know translation doesn't mean one-to-one subtitle to spoken word, still, over time one would learn, I hope, corresponding Japanese words for their English translations). For example, Luffy's famous line:

I always thought "Kaizoku-ō" meant King (because it sounds like German for emperorer: "Kaiser"), but actually it means Pirate!

So I have just started a project: mimikara, which only shows the English subtitle at the exact time the audio is spoken

It works by turning a Japanese and an English subtitle file into .ass subtitles where each English word fades in at the moment its Japanese counterpart is spoken, by pairing the cues by time, tokenising the Japanese with UniDic, asking an LLM which English word maps to which Japanese word, timing those words by mora count or forced alignment, and rendering the line with colour and opacity changes only so the English still reads naturally left to right.

Wall Street's Biggest Bubble Looks Ready to Burst, and I Don't Mean AI or Quantum Computing

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A $1.5 trillion powder keg can upend the stock market’s four-year rally.

Last month, Wall Street and investors celebrated the fourth anniversary of the current bull market. Both the benchmark S&P 500 (^GSPC +0.31%) and growth-fueled Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC +1.09%) have more than doubled from their 2022 bear market lows, while the iconic Dow Jones Industrial Average (^DJI -0.46%) has rallied as much as 90%.

A confluence of factors has propelled Wall Street's major stock indexes to new heights, no two of which are larger than the evolution of artificial intelligence (AI) and the advent and early-stage proliferation of quantum computing. Both are trillion-dollar addressable opportunities that investors simply don't want to miss.

But following the stock market's parabolic climb, concerns are mounting about a possible AI and/or quantum computing bubble-bursting event. While history hasn't exactly been kind to next-big-thing technologies -- every game-changing technology has endured an early-innings bubble-bursting event since the mid-1990s -- there's an even more prominent bubble that looks ready to burst, based on what history tells us.

While history shows that the AI and quantum computing bubbles should eventually burst, the $1.5 trillion outstanding margin debt bubble may be the spark that upends the stock market's four-year rally.

"Margin" is money an investor borrows from their broker, with interest, to wager against (short-sell) or purchase securities. When margin is used to buy stocks or exchange-traded funds (ETFs), it represents a form of leverage.

Using margin can be risky. If an investor's wager moves in the desired direction, they can quickly amplify their gains. But if a stock or ETF moves in the opposite direction, losses can be quickly magnified. This dynamic makes outstanding margin debt a somewhat crude, yet intriguing measure of investors' willingness to take risks.

Total Margin Debt hits $1.5 Trillion, a new all-time high 🤯 👀 https://t.co/1IXqZGgrqs

According to FINRA, outstanding margin debt rocketed to an all-time high of $1.502 trillion in June. Although margin debt is expected to steadily increase over time in lockstep with the overall value of the stock market, bad things often happen when the amount investors borrow from their brokers rises dramatically over a short time frame.

Over roughly three decades, there have been four instances in which outstanding margin debt has climbed by at least 65% over a reasonably short timeline (12 to 19 months), including the present. The previous three occurrences all coincided with substantial bear markets:

History makes it crystal clear that outsize risk-taking is the ultimate red flag on Wall Street. While margin debt can't tell us when the music will stop or what catalysts could cause the stock market to sink, it clearly shows the unsustainability of excess risk-taking, which is doubly problematic in a historically expensive stock market.

From a historical standpoint, the short-term outlook for Wall Street's AI- and quantum computing-driven rally isn't pretty. If history rhymes once more, investors can expect a sharp bear market downturn in the presumed not-too-distant future.

While deep red arrows are known to tug on investors' heartstrings, significant pullbacks in Wall Street's major stock indexes can also represent generational buying opportunities for optimistic, long-term investors.

Despite most investors disliking stock market corrections and bear markets, these events are a natural and healthy part of the investing cycle. Given that downturns are commonly driven by emotions, there's not much that can be done from a monetary or fiscal policy perspective to prevent double-digit declines from occurring.

The silver lining for investors is that bull and bear market cycles aren't linear.

A May 2026 post on X (formerly Twitter) from Bespoke Investment Group examined every S&P 500 bull and bear market since the start of the Great Depression in September 1929. What researchers found was a mile-wide gap between bull and bear markets.

The current bull market that began on 10/12/22 is now the 9th longest in S&P 500 history, surpassing the 1,324-day bull that ended on 2/9/1966: https://t.co/4mGsS2t2ft

On one hand, the typical S&P 500 bear market found its trough after an average of 286 calendar days (roughly 9.5 months). By comparison, the average S&P 500 bull market persisted for 1,023 calendar days, or approximately 3.6 times as long as the average bear market.

More than a century of backward-looking S&P 500 total return data from Crestmont Research provides even more for patient investors to cheer about.

Analysts at Crestmont calculated the rolling 20-year total returns, including dividends, of the S&P 500 since 1900. This meant tracking its components in other major indexes prior to the S&P's official inception in 1923.

Crestmont's analysis yielded 107 rolling 20-year periods (1900-1919, 1901-1920, and so on, through 2006-2025), all of which produced a positive average annual total return. I repeat, 107 of 107 rolling 20-year periods generated a profit, including dividends.

Even if someone hypothetically invested at a stock market peak, or during a recession, depression, war, pandemic, etc., they would have always made money if they held an S&P 500-tracking index for 20 years.

Patience is a virtue and a path to potential riches on Wall Street. If the $1.5 trillion margin debt bubble bursts, consider it an opportunity to pounce.

Sean Williams has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

*Average returns of all recommendations since inception. Cost basis and return based on previous market day close.

È il momento di Pinamonti: titolare dopo 2 mesi. Ma Gattuso non lo ha mai 'abbandonato'

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

La più grande qualità dimostrata finora da Gennaro Gattuso è stata senza dubbio la capacità di recuperare giocatori finiti ai margini del progetto, restituendo loro fiducia e permettendo alla Lazio di ampliare le proprie soluzioni. Tra questi c'è anche Andrea Pinamonti, mai messo da parte dal tecnico calabrese nonostante un avvio di stagione complicato, che lo aveva visto scivolare indietro nelle gerarchie alle spalle di Noslin. A pesare era stata soprattutto una condizione fisica non ottimale, con cui l'attaccante si era presentato nella Capitale dopo un'estate da separato in casa a Sassuolo. Gattuso, però, ha sempre ribadito di voler contare su tutti i suoi uomini. Gattuso si è affidato a lui per sostituire capitan Zaccagni, spostando Noslin sulla sinistra.

Nonostante un minutaggio finora non particolarmente elevato, Gennaro Gattuso si è sempre dimostrato un estimatore di Pinamonti, difendendolo a più riprese pubblicamente. Anche oggi, nel pre partita, il tecnico ha ribadito: "Lui deve giocare, non deve pensare alla pressione. Deve metterci entusiasmo, andare con veemenza e aiutarci ma ce l'ha: è un giocatore che sta migliorando tantissimo e quando vedo che è anche una persona per bene siamo un bel pezzo avanti".

Is It Too Late to Plant Amaryllis for Christmas? Here's How to Get Gorgeous Blooms in Time

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Plant These 7 Flower Seeds in November for Gorgeous Early Blooms Next Year

The clock is ticking, but there's still hope for a festive flower show. Choosing the right bulbs and growing conditions can make all the difference.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

It’s not necessarily too late to plant amaryllis bulbs for Christmas, but timing does matter. Amaryllis typically blooms six to eight weeks after planting, so early to mid-November is your last chance to get bulbs in the pot if you want striking holiday flowers in time for the big day.

If you’re growing amaryllis for holiday flowers, choose the right type. Amaryllis usually refers to Hippeastrum, a South American native, not true Amaryllis, also known as belladonna lilies from South Africa. Most Hippeastrum bulbs sold this time of year are grown in the Southern Hemisphere and will bloom in six to eight weeks. Dutch-grown bulbs take longer, so avoid these.

Flowering also varies by the plant’s growing conditions, which means you have some control. While early or mid-November is the ideal time to plant a holiday amaryllis, you can do a few things to speed up the process if you’ve just missed the window.

Getting healthy holiday blooms isn’t just luck. How you choose and plant your bulbs is important. Start by selecting the best bulbs. Look for large, firm, and healthy bulbs without soft spots, damage, or mold. If you see a bulb that has the tip of a green stem emerging, grab it. This bulb has already begun to break dormancy and should flower sooner.

While unusual varieties can be expensive, you don't need to spend a lot of money on bulbs – Walmart sells preplanted bulbs for under $6.

Next, choose a good-quality potting mix, such as Miracle-Gro Indoor Potting Mix, available at Amazon, and a container with drainage holes that is only one or two inches (2.5 to 5 cm) wider than the bulb. Position the bulb in the pot so that the pointy end is up and one-third to one-half of the bulb is above the surface of the potting mix.

Water your planted bulb thoroughly, letting it all drain through, before setting it in a warm, bright spot. Keep the potting mix just slightly damp, never soggy. Amaryllis doesn’t need much water and can rot in soggy conditions.

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

While you can’t significantly alter the timeline for flowering, a few easy tips encourage strong and steady growth:

You can’t do a lot to force it, but these conditions provide everything your plant needs for a healthy growth rate. Just don’t overdo it. Fertilizer, too much heat, and too much water can do more harm than good.

Be realistic about timing with amaryllis bulbs. If it’s late November, you might still have time for a Christmas flower. Choose the largest, healthiest bulb, with a bit of green growth, if possible, for the best start. Provide the best conditions and cross your fingers.

If it’s already December, you won’t get a flower by Christmas from a dormant bulb. However, you can probably find a potted amaryllis that has already broken dormancy and has a stem. This plant will likely bloom by Christmas.

You got some gorgeous blooms in time for Christmas, but now you want them to last. It’s time to switch your care strategy from encouraging growth to keeping flowers looking their best.

When the flowers do fade, snip them off to keep the plant looking tidy. Once all the flowers are done blooming, cut the stem back close to the bulb. With the right care, you should enjoy healthy, attractive flowers for two to three weeks.

Here are a few great options available to buy now, with beautiful blooms that will bring a festive splash of color to your home.

Whether you get flowers in time for Christmas or not, forcing an amaryllis bulb indoors is a great winter activity. Enjoy your blooms, even if they don’t open until January. And if you’re up for it, you can keep the plant going and encourage it to bloom again next year.

Throwing 70,480 CPU cores at Azeroth so you don't have to stare at a login queue

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Blizzard pulled out all the stops to ensure World of Warcraft: Forever survived a chaotic launch night. What was the company's secret weapon? Splurging an absurd amount of money on infrastructure made up of 3,924 virtual machines with 70,480 CPU cores and a whopping 251,136 GB, or 251 TB, of memory.

Praised by media and players as one of the smoothest launches in Blizzard's history, World of Warcraft: Forever requires significant computing firepower. 3,924 virtual machines work out to 18 cores and 64GB of memory per instance. Or, the company could be using a mixture of 16-core and 32-core nodes with slightly less memory.

To put the infrastructure's scale in consumer terms, we are looking at about 8,810 mainstream processors, or 7,848 high-end gaming systems with 32GB of memory each. In any event, the hardware is greatly improved compared to what World of Warcraft used to run on back in 2009.

*Slaps Roof of WoW: Forever* This baby is running on: 3,924 Virtual Machines 251,136 GB of Memory 70,480 CPU Cores https://t.co/P9PdRBzke7November 5, 2026

In 2009, Blizzard said it used 10 data centers worldwide to host World of Warcraft. At the time, the infrastructure included 13,250 server blades, totaling 75,000 CPU cores and 112.5 TB of memory. By comparison, World of Warcraft: Forever leverages 6% fewer CPU cores but rocks 2.2X more memory. Modern processors are leagues faster than chips from 17 years ago. By today's standards, the bottleneck is memory capacity and bandwidth, which is why the infrastructure can run on fewer cores but has more than double the memory to compensate.

Blizzard does not specify the exact hardware that World of Warcraft: Forever runs on. However, if we were to translate the core specifications to, for example, AMD's EPYC 128-core Bergamo or 96-core Genoa chips, it could be anywhere between 275 and 365 2U server nodes, depending on which processor it uses. Therefore, World of Warcraft: Forever could be running on just eight to ten 42U server racks, a noteworthy improvement over 17 years ago.

Blizzard should have no trouble building the infrastructure for World of Warcraft: Forever. A ballpark estimate for about ten 42U server racks with EPYC chips is at least $15 million, even with today's memory pricing. But then again, since Microsoft owns Blizzard now, the company could just be tapping its parent's existing Azure platform. Technically, it is not free compute, but Blizzard would benefit from cost pricing and priority allocation of Azure's capacity.

World of Warcraft: Forever shows that a high-profile launch does not have to be a nightmare when companies are willing to invest in computing infrastructure. In this case, many would agree Blizzard is putting those 251TB to good use, although Sam Altman may say otherwise.

Person of Interest

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

home • blog • poesía • photography • say hi 👋

A few days ago, I started watching Person of Interest, and I haven’t been able to stop. Aside from the fact that the show feels even more relevant today than it did 15 years ago, when it first aired, it’s brought back so many memories of the early 2010s, when I first started getting into tech and developing an interest in computers.

It’s reminded me of all the forums and online communities I used to be part of as a teenager, the whole SOPA and PIPA controversy, the shutdown of Megaupload, and just what the Internet was like back then. I also find it pretty crazy and, frankly, a little sad to think that something like the Machine, which seemed like pure science fiction at the time, isn't all that far from our reality today.

But beyond all that, watching it has made me incredibly nostalgic, and considering it’s already 15 years old, I think it’s aged remarkably well.

Hace unos días empecé a ver Person of Interest y no he podido parar. Más allá de que la serie se siente incluso más actual que hace 15 años, cuando se estrenó, me ha hecho recordar mucho los primeros años de la década de 2010, que fue cuando empecé a meterme en el mundillo informático y a interesarme por los computadores.

Me ha traído muchos recuerdos de los foros y las comunidades online en las que participaba siendo adolescente, de todo el rollo de SOPA y PIPA, del cierre de Megaupload y, en general, de cómo era Internet en aquella época. También me parece muy loco y, siendo franco, un poco triste pensar que algo como la Máquina, que en aquel entonces parecía sacado de la ciencia ficción, hoy no esté tan lejos de nuestra realidad.

Pero, más allá de eso, verla me ha despertado mucha nostalgia y, teniendo en cuenta que ya tiene 15 años, me parece que ha envejecido bastante bien

Gold is still 25% below its peak even as demand reaches $188B in Q3

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

October's losses show why knowing who's buying gold is only part of understanding its price.

Central banks bought more gold in the third quarter of 2026 than consumers worldwide bought for jewelry.

According to the World Gold Council's latest demand report, central banks and other official institutions acquired an estimated net 321 metric tons, while jewelry consumption came to 313 tons.

In the same quarter a year earlier, jewelry buyers had consumed about 150 tons more than central banks acquired.

By September, the institutions buying gold for their reserves had overtaken the people buying it to wear, although roughly 63% of estimated official demand couldn't be traced through publicly reported country-level purchases.

The price barely kept pace with Bitcoin, which gained 42.7% during the quarter versus gold's 3.84%. Both attracted money through exchange-traded funds, so this wasn't a straightforward case of investors ditching one for the other. They bought both and got different results.

However, Bitcoin's lead came crashing down in October: gold fell 6.3%, while Bitcoin lost 7.3%. Governments kept accumulating bullion, and investors kept believing in scarce assets, but neither belief did much for that month's returns.

The buyers behind those beliefs, and their reasons for holding on, explain rather more than the safe-haven label does.

Global gold demand reached $188 billion in the third quarter, up 22% from a year earlier. That sounds like an impressive shopping spree until you weigh the purchases.

Buyers took home 1% less metal, at 1,369 tons, while the average quarterly price was 23% higher. Much of the extra spending covered the price increase rather than buying more gold.

That was especially apparent in jewelry, where consumption fell 17% to 313 tons, the weakest third quarter in the council's records since 2000. Buyers in China and India cut purchases or chose lighter and lower-carat pieces, adjusting what they bought to what they could afford.

Physical investment wasn't immune either: demand for bars and coins fell 19% to 268 tons. Paying more per ounce didn't make every buyer more eager to own one.

Central banks' net purchases rose 42% to 321 tons, while gold-backed ETFs added 211 tons, reversing the previous quarter's withdrawals.

Those purchases helped offset weaker demand from jewelry buyers and physical investors, giving the market support from buyers who wanted gold as a financial holding even as others balked at the cost.

The categories don't divide neatly into institutions buying and households retreating. Individuals own gold through ETFs, and institutions can own bars and coins.

But the numbers do show that demand weakened in traditional consumer and physical-investment markets, while official reserves and listed funds absorbed more metal. Gold was becoming a harder purchase to justify at the counter and an easier allocation to defend in a portfolio.

Of the estimated 321 tons in net purchases, only about 120 tons appeared in publicly reported country-level activity.

China disclosed additions of 63 tons and Poland 40 tons, making them the largest reported buyers, but the country disclosures leave approximately 201 tons of the council's estimate unaccounted for in the public figures.

Reporting delays and purchases governments don't disclose can explain activity missing from the country data, and the broader estimate may be revised. The gap alone also doesn't establish secret, coordinated purchases.

We have a much better estimate of the total than a complete account of who bought it, which limits how confidently we can interpret the buying.

Q3 2026 net official demand, using rounded inputs: 321 metric tons estimated in total, of which 120 were publicly reported. The remaining 201 are calculated by subtraction, not added to the total. Unreported activity can include delayed or undisclosed purchases; estimates may be revised.

Source: World Gold Council, Q3 2026 central-bank demand

Central banks acquired an estimated 675 tons in the first nine months of 2026, exceeding their purchases over the same period of 2025.

Their appetite is large enough to offset weakness elsewhere, but incomplete disclosures make it harder to attribute that appetite to particular governments or motives. The survey evidence offers more detail on the latter, with some important limits of its own.

Quarterly totals. Jewelry measures consumption; bars and coins, ETFs, and official institutions measure net demand. Negative ETF values mean net withdrawals. Official institutions include central banks. These selected channels do not exhaust total gold demand and do not separate household from institutional investors.

Source: World Gold Council / Metals Focus / Refinitiv GFMS, Q3 2026, Table 1

In the World Gold Council's 2026 central-bank survey, 74% of respondents expected the US dollar's share of global reserves to be lower in five years. That gives some basis for the argument that reserve managers want less dependence on the dollar.

The IMF's second-quarter foreign-exchange reserve data put the dollar's share at 56.7%, down from 57.18% in the preceding quarter. It remained the dominant reserve currency, with dollar holdings largely steady as holdings in other currencies expanded.

This series also excludes monetary gold, so it can't tell us how much gold replaced dollars.

Even the funding of gold purchases is less straightforward than the familiar sell-Treasuries-buy-bullion argument allows. Half the survey respondents said they'd finance new gold purchases through domestic programs using local currency, while 38% said they'd sell existing reserve assets.

Buying locally produced gold with local currency doesn't require a corresponding sale of US government debt, and the quarterly demand totals can't identify what financed every purchase.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Reserve managers can want more gold without wanting out of everything else. Bullion gives them an asset whose value doesn't depend on an issuer honoring a financial obligation, making it a way to reduce reliance on other governments' currencies and debt.

However, the bargain still includes a market price, and that price can fall enough to make the word “protection” feel rather generous over shorter periods.

Gold finished October at $3,913 an ounce, down 6.3% for the month and roughly 25% below its January peak, according to the World Gold Council's November market commentary.

Central bank demand hadn't spared holders that loss, because even a persistent buyer can't dictate the price everyone else trades at. Futures positions and the need to raise cash can overwhelm the reassurance of a government adding bullion to its reserves.

Global gold-backed ETFs attracted $2.7 billion in net inflows during October, adding 19 tons and taking holdings to a record 4,275 tons. Their combined assets under management still fell 6% to $542 billion, as the lower gold price more than offset the new money.

The funds held more metal and were worth less, an entirely possible outcome that gets lost when inflows are treated as a verdict on returns.

The broader point is consistent with a July 2026 IMF study, which found that gold's ability to hedge market risk depends on the shock and the economic conditions around it. Gold can help diversify a reserve portfolio over time without reliably sheltering it during extreme stress.

Someone reducing exposure to sovereign credit over several years and someone trying to avoid a loss this month may buy the same asset, but they're asking it to do different jobs.

Bitcoin gave investors a much better third quarter, gaining 42.7% to end September around $83,570, according to 21Shares' quarterly analysis.

US spot Bitcoin ETFs added about 79,800 BTC, worth about $6.2 billion, reversing roughly $4.8 billion in second-quarter withdrawals.

Their purchases were nearly twice the estimated 41,300 BTC newly mined over the period, giving the rally a measurable source of demand through funds available to professional and individual investors alike.

That comparison with new supply is striking, although newly mined coins are only one source of Bitcoin available to buy. Existing holders own a far larger stock, and some were reducing their positions while ETFs accumulated.

21Shares estimated that balances in the long-term-holder cohort fell by 248,000 BTC during the quarter. The figure isn't a direct count of exchange sales, since coins can leave the cohort through transfers or other classification effects, but it prevents ETF buying from being mistaken for a market in which everyone was adding to their holdings.

Global gold-backed funds attracted a record $31 billion in third-quarter inflows and added 211 tons of bullion, even as the metal's price gain fell well short of Bitcoin's.

The dollar totals cover different markets: the gold figure is global, while the Bitcoin figure covers US spot ETFs. Their respective market sizes and investor bases differ too, so treating the totals as a head-to-head contest would tell us little.

Investors added exposure to both assets, but their quarterly returns differed by nearly 39%, which offers little support for a simple account of Bitcoin rallying because money was leaving gold. It also limits what ETF data alone can explain.

Listed funds supplied demand in both markets, while trading elsewhere, including derivatives positions and borrowed money, helped determine how far each price moved.

October narrowed that enormous performance gap in an unwelcome way. Bitcoin fell 7.3% to about $77,500, while gold lost 6.3% over the same month.

Reported October 2026 returns in US dollars. Gold uses the LBMA PM benchmark through Oct. 30, the last business day; Bitcoin trades continuously. These are price returns, excluding fees and income, rather than ETF returns. Similar losses in one month do not establish a lasting correlation or a common cause.

Source: World Gold Council / Bloomberg / LBMA, October 2026, Table 1 · 21Shares, November 2026 Bitcoin outlook

Gold ETFs continued to attract net investment globally, led by Europe and Asia, while North American funds recorded modest withdrawals. On Nov. 4, Reuters reported that the largest Bitcoin-backed funds tracked by LSEG/Lipper lost $215 million in October.

While the totals don't establish that investors moved money from Bitcoin to gold, they show that net buying persisted across global bullion funds while the tracked Bitcoin funds lost money to withdrawals.

The World Gold Council pointed to reductions in speculative positions, revised expectations for Federal Reserve policy, and rotation between assets as possible contributors to gold's decline.

Bitcoin contended with weaker tech stocks, tighter credit conditions, the Binance hack, and the US midterm elections. Different sellers had different reasons to act, even though holders of both assets ended the month with losses.

Scarcity offers little defense against someone who needs cash or wants less exposure to volatile markets. Central-bank purchases can absorb gold without guaranteeing its price, just as Bitcoin's supply limit can constrain issuance without guaranteeing buyers at the price its holders would prefer.

October put both propositions on display, even while some investors continued to add bullion to their portfolios.

The sovereign comparison needs a careful approach, because a government owning Bitcoin doesn't necessarily mean it has entered the market to buy it.

The US established a Strategic Bitcoin Reserve in March 2025, formally giving Bitcoin a place among assets the federal government can retain and manage.

However, the executive order creating the reserve directed that it be funded primarily with Bitcoin already held through criminal or civil asset forfeitures.

Officials were also authorized to devise budget-neutral ways to acquire additional Bitcoin without incremental taxpayer costs. That permission and the decision to retain existing coins have policy importance, but neither establishes a recurring purchase program comparable with central banks adding gold to their reserves.

That's why putting seized Bitcoin into a reserve can happen without buying a single additional coin on the market.

The World Gold Council's quarterly figures estimate net additions to official gold holdings. The US reserve order governs, in large part, what happens to assets the government already owns.

Gold already has an established role in central bank balance sheets and reserve management. Bitcoin's newer sovereign role could evolve, but ETF access, government recognition, and official purchases each affect demand through a different route.

Investors can't assume progress on one will produce the buying associated with another, however attractive the reserve announcement sounds.

Interest-rate expectations and access to cash will continue to test both markets. Stronger US employment or inflation readings can encourage expectations of tighter Federal Reserve policy, increasing the appeal of interest-bearing assets relative to gold and potentially making financial conditions less accommodating for Bitcoin.

Weaker readings can support expectations of easier policy, although a deterioration severe enough to prompt fears of an economic slowdown can also leave investors wanting cash.

Neither the gold-demand report nor Bitcoin's ETF inflows can settle how investors will react to the next release. They can tell us more about who has been buying and how readily those buyers might leave.

Central banks can accumulate gold over several years, while ETF investors can cut exposure much faster. Consumers can buy lighter jewelry, and traders using borrowed money can face demands to reduce positions precisely when they'd prefer to wait.

That mix helps explain why persistent belief in an asset can coexist with a sharp fall in its price.

The reserve manager diversifying away from another government's obligations may have little reason to sell bullion during a correction, while an investor facing losses elsewhere may sell the same metal because it's readily tradable.

Bitcoin's fixed supply doesn't remove that need for cash, and official recognition doesn't give its holders the resources or mandate to ride out every downturn.

Central banks overtaking jewelry buyers shows how gold demand is being redistributed, and October's losses show the limits of what those purchases can promise other holders.

Someone buying for protection needs the asset's behavior to fit the risk they're trying to reduce and the period they can afford to hold it.

Gold can serve a reserve manager well over years and disappoint an investor over weeks, while Bitcoin can deliver a spectacular quarter without becoming a dependable place to wait out the next sell-off.

Gino Matos is a law school graduate and a seasoned journalist with six years of experience in the crypto industry. His expertise primarily focuses on the Brazilian blockchain…

Doom delivers five minutes of terminal violence on an IBM mainframe

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Browser demo renders the classic shooter in ASCII, with no login required and considerable squinting encouraged

Can an IBM Z mainframe run Doom? Of course it can. The more interesting question is whether you can play it through a text terminal without ruining your eyesight.

WND Software's browser demo connects to an IBM Z mainframe over SSH using its terminal emulator. Wojciech Graj's DOOM-ASCII supplies the text-based carnage, replacing conventional graphics with colored characters. There's no sound.

It is, however, completely playable (although you might need to recalibrate your vision to work out what is going on, particularly with regard to distant objects). Sessions are limited to five minutes, with room for up to 50 simultaneous users, but we had no trouble getting in. ASCII also presented no issues once the game was in motion. This writer played the original shareware release on an Amstrad ALT386SX portable, so squinting at an indistinct demon felt reassuringly familiar.

That Doom can be persuaded to run on an IBM Z mainframe is not particularly noteworthy. The code has turned up everywhere from databases to PCB designers. Even the humble Pi Pico was roped into the fun.

Keeping the action playable through a text terminal is the more entertaining achievement. A year ago, an IBM consultant persuaded Doom to run on IBM Z and LinuxONE using VNC to bring the pixelated mayhem to life. Using a terminal emulator, while a neat demonstration of the capabilities of WND's software, is perhaps more an example of "because I can" masochism.

It's undeniably an enjoyable way to burn through five minutes. No installation or login required – just a willingness to shoot at punctuation. ®

I’ve been tracking Garmin prices for years — here are 5 deals I’d be holding out for this Black Friday

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The prices of the best Garmin watches have risen considerably in recent years, but if you wait for big sales periods like Black Friday, you can almost always get a great deal on the brand’s products.

Black Friday is now just a few weeks away, and I’m expecting widespread deals on Garmin watches to start appearing soon. There are a few good discounts available already, like the Garmin Fenix 8 dropping to $742 at Amazon at the time of writing — but I think the best is yet to come.

I’ve been testing Garmin watches for a decade and tracking their prices during sales like Black Friday, and I’ve picked five I expect to see heavily discounted in the coming weeks.

Garmin launched two new entry-level running watches this year: the Forerunner 70 and Forerunner 170; the latter is a direct replacement for the Forerunner 165.

That means the Forerunner 165 is set to be a star of upcoming sales, and indeed it’s already reduced from $249 to $202 at Best Buy.

However, the Forerunner 165 has dropped as low as $129 this year, and that’s the price I’m hoping it will be available for in the Black Friday sales, so I’ll hold off for now.

The Forerunner 265 is an attractive AMOLED multi-sport watch that offers a more affordable alternative to newer models like the Forerunner 570.

Its lowest price ever is $299, a deal that popped up in last year’s holiday sales period, so hopefully we’ll see that deal emerge again in the coming weeks.

No one is offering a really good deal on the Forerunner 265 right now, which for me suggests it’s primed for widespread discounts soon.

The Garmin Forerunner 965 is another older watch you can expect to see on sale, with $449 being its lowest price to date, and what I’d hope it drops to this year, at least.

It’s a fantastic triathlon watch with maps and a bright AMOLED display, and at $449 it would be $300 cheaper than the newer Garmin Forerunner 970’s full price.

If you want a watch right now, the black Forerunner 965 is currently $479 at Amazon, which is a great deal, but I think it could still drop lower.

The Garmin Epix Pro has now been superseded by several AMOLED Fenix models in the Fenix 8, Fenix 8 Pro and Fenix 9, but it’s still an excellent adventure watch and likely to be hundreds of dollars cheaper than those newer models on sale.

You can already save $360 on the Epix Pro, which is $639 at Amazon, but I’d be holding out for a deal that brings the watch down to at least $499, the lowest price it’s been in the past.

If you want a more up-to-date adventure watch, the Fenix 8 Pro has only recently been replaced by the Fenix 9 Pro as the flagship option in Garmin’s line-up, so it will hopefully be getting big discounts in Black Friday sales.

The lowest price the Fenix 8 Pro has been in the past is $849, and that’s the price I think it will drop to during the Black Friday sales.

You can get the Garmin Fenix 8 Pro for $1,049 at Amazon at the moment, a $50 saving, but I certainly think a better deal will come along if you wait a week or two.

"Dobbiamo avere coraggio, non preoccuparsi della velocità..."

Prima del match di oggi contro il Napoli, Gennaro Gattuso ha rilasciato delle dichiarazioni ai microfoni di Dazn.

Bisogna riuscire a mantenere l'entusiasmo e la voglia. La classifica non si vede. Oggi è una partita difficilissima, vengono da un periodo negativo ma hanno qualità. Per noi è una grande prova. GSMA MWC26 Doha opens with call to align on global industry standards and close MENA's connectivity gap

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Operators, technology leaders, policymakers and innovators gather in Doha today for three days of exhibition, collaboration and debate on the future of connectivity across the Middle East and North Africa

DOHA, Qatar, Nov. 8, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- MWC26 Doha, held in partnership with Qatar's Ministry of Communications and Information Technology (MCIT), opened today at Doha Exhibition and Convention Centre (DECC), bringing together connectivity ecosystem to shape the next phase of growth for a mobile economy forecast to add $470 billion to the Middle East and North Africa by 2030.

MWC26 Doha officially opened today, in presence of His Excellency Sheikh Saoud bin Abdulrahman bin Hassan bin Ali Al Thani, Deputy Prime Minister and Minister of State for Defence Affairs. His Excellency Mr. Mohammed bin Ali Al Mannai, Minister of Communications and Information Technology, State of Qatar delivered a welcome address.

In his opening remarks, H.E. Mr. Mohammed bin Ali Al Mannai, Minister of Communications and Information Technology, said: "The second edition of MWC Doha takes place at a time when the requirements of telecommunications infrastructure are undergoing fundamental change. As artificial intelligence advances towards executing tasks through intelligent agents, the need for more resilient networks and advanced computing capabilities continues to grow. At the same time, global challenges underscore the importance of diversifying connectivity routes and ensuring service continuity."

GSMA Director General Vivek Badrinath and ITU Secretary-General Doreen Bogdan-Martin took to the stage for a fireside chat. Mr Badrinath set out the key priorities shaping the region's connected future, with a continued commitment to global standards keeping networks and services interoperable across markets, as well as a renewed push to extend connectivity to populations that remain offline in MENA.

Vivek Badrinath, Director General, GSMA, said: "Progress only counts if everyone shares in it. That means two things: global standards that keep networks, services and devices working across borders, and a concerted effort to connect the people who are still offline. Over the next three days, MWC Doha, alongside its co-location with ITU's PP-26, will bring together the policy makers, innovators and industry leaders setting these standards and building these networks, to help make this vision a reality. As the ITU Plenipotentiary continues, we are confident that the enhanced dialog which our co-location has allowed will feed into the important conversations taking place."

Mikel Arteta admits Arsenal are struggling with familiar problem after Hull win

📅 2026-11-08  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Mikel Arteta admits Arsenal are struggling with an old problem that became so cliched that it was featured in a sit-com 18 years ago.

The Gunners beat Hull City 1-0 on Saturday in the Premier League, with Martin Odegaard scoring the only goal at the Emirates.

Any problem Arsenal are struggling with is not too serious as that win, followed by Manchester City’s defeat at Nottingham Forest, put Arteta’s men six points clear at the top of the table.

However, Arteta was frustrated with Arsenal only scoring one goal and it not coming until the 72nd minute, despite dominating possession and producing 21 shots at goal.

Sign up to our daily football newsletter, pick your team and get the latest news sent directly to your inbox every morning.

Sometimes the inability to even produce a shot was the problem, despite having the ball in dangerous areas plenty of times.

In a 2008 episode of The IT Crowd, Moss and Roy both try to ingratiate with football fans by learning the phrase: ‘The thing about Arsenal is they’re always trying to walk it in.’

Arteta seems to have a similar take on his team, accusing them of trying to score the ‘perfect goal’ against defensively-minded teams.

‘[We need to] be more efficient,’ the Arsenal manager said. ‘We are one against one against the keeper. That’s happened twice today. There is a penalty there, there is another situation inside the box where the ball is bouncing.

‘So many shots that have been deflected, blocked. We need to find ways to be more efficient, sometimes even more pragmatic.

Use AI to go deeper into the stories you care about – powered by Metro and trusted publications.

‘Sometimes my feeling is that we are trying to score a perfect goal against these low blocks. We need to be much more chaotic than that, and that’s it. And open, I think, the scoreline much earlier because obviously that’s going to change the course of the game.

‘We had enough chances to make the scoreline much bigger. We weren’t efficient enough to do that. And then we have to rely on generating volume, generating a lot of situations in and around the box, insisting, insisting and insisting until we are able to break them down. And this is exactly how we won the game.’

Odegaard is Arsenal’s top-scorer this season with five goals in all competitions and three in the Premier League.

While Arsenal’s attack does not always penetrate as much as Arteta would like, the defence is incredibly reliable.

Another clean sheet meant the Gunners have conceded just five goals in 10 league games and have not conceded in 10 of their 17 matches across all competitions.

Centre-back Gabriel Magalhaes said: ‘If we don’t concede goals, we have a big chance to win games. We are really happy to have another clean sheet.

‘Like I said, when we have a strong team, a strong defence, we don’t concede goals. I think this season, how many is that? Ten clean sheets in the whole competition? This is our standard. We need to keep winning.’

From 'Red Sprouts' to school board races, DSA's years-long effort to reach children gains momentum

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

American Parents Coalition’s Alleigh Marré warns DSA-backed efforts are embedding socialist ideology in childcare, classrooms and school boards to shape children’s worldviews as early as preschool.

The Democratic Socialists of America (DSA) is taking its message into classrooms, child-care programs and school boards, urging organizers to approach child care "from a socialist perspective" through initiatives such as Socialist Sprouts, and its been an initiative for years, a parental-rights watchdog warns.

In New York City, the DSA's "Red Sprouts" program held a training session in October 2026 to equip organizers to provide "resistance-driven childcare" at demonstrations, meetings and community events.

The training encouraged participants to incorporate political themes into childcare through "radical storytelling", "cooperative games" and age-appropriate discussions.

"There's been a real influx in enthusiasm and their ability and desire to recruit and grow their movement," American Parents Coalition leader Alleigh Marré told Fox News Digital. "What's particularly concerning and insidious, is their direct efforts to apply that to not just adults, but to youth."

Marré said the American Parents Coalition is working to equip parents with resources to identify and question political activism in schools, including through its "Lookout" initiative.

New York City Democratic Socialists hold a rally in Union Square on Nov. 16, 2025, to mark the start of a campaign to tax the rich and win universal childcare. (Selcuk Acar/Anadolu)

PARENTS SHOULDN’T NEED THE HELP OF CONGRESS TO SEE THEIR CHILDREN’S SCHOOL CURRICULUM

The New York City DSA chapter also established Comrades With Kids, a family-focused organizing group that seeks to be a "political and social home base for working-class families." The group posted photos of DSA-themed coloring pages for children provided at a Zohran for Parents event in September 2025.

The New York initiatives are part of a larger effort by the DSA to incorporate childcare and political education into its organizing activities.

For years, the DSA's National Political Education Committee has offered training on approaching childcare for activists "from a socialist perspective", including activities for children at organizing events. In 2025, the committee outlined plans to develop a "Socialist Sprouts" curriculum geared for children, parents and caregivers involved in the movement.

The organization's efforts to introduce socialist ideas to children date back at least a decade.

In 2016, New York City DSA members launched a Socialist Sunday School for children ages 2 to 6. The program used political songs such as "Union Maid" and "Solidarity Forever," alongside children's books, including "The Lorax," to introduce discussions about corporate greed, environmentalism and organizing against unfairness.

In Ohio, the Cleveland DSA chapter created a children's coloring book to "help kids learn what we work on at DSA." Its illustrations include a version of "The Three Little Pigs" tied to the chapter's housing agenda.

Items on a table during an election night party held by the Democratic Socialists of America - LA chapter at The Greyhound on Tuesday, Nov. 4, 2025 in Los Angeles, Calif. (Eric Thayer / Los Angeles Times via Getty Images)

POLITICAL ACTIVISM, ANTISEMITISM ALLEGATIONS PUT PUBLIC SCHOOLS UNDER THE MICROSCOPE

The DSA's online store has also sold children's clothing promoting a "Green New Deal for Public Schools."

The DSA describes much of its childcare programming as a way to make political organizing more accessible to working-class families. Marré, however, argued that introducing socialist ideas to young children raises concerns about political indoctrination.

"The DSA really understands and values that if you can indoctrinate and teach these kids really young, their perceived value of, of socialism, that that's going to stick with kids for a lifetime," Marre said. "It's much easier to teach a child an idea or ideal in their infancy, and when they're very young and they're forming their worldview, and we have seen many efforts to try and capitalize on that."

"There's this really concerted effort to instill an ideology in really, really young children, rather than teaching kids how to think and introducing a breadth of ideologies of religious interests, etc.," Marré said. "It's really focused on socialist and communist aligned ideas."

Beyond its childcare programs, the DSA has also pursued a strategy to expand its influence over public education through local school board elections.

At its 2023 national convention, the DSA adopted an amendment prioritizing the recruitment and support of socialist school board candidates. Rose DuBois, one of the amendment's authors, argued that school board elections could help the organization reach "new kinds of voters, such as parents," while building the infrastructure needed to win higher-profile races.

Zohran Mamdani, New York City mayoral candidate, during a campaign rally at United Palace in New York, US, on Monday, Oct. 13, 2025. (Christian Monterrosa/Bloomberg via Getty Images)

MAMDANI-BACKED SOCIALIST WINS NYC HOUSE SEAT AS DSA EXPANDS ITS REACH IN CONGRESS

The Los Angeles chapter of the DSA established its own Public Education Committee, with priorities that include expanding the organization's influence on school boards, recruiting school board candidates, working with teachers unions and promoting policies such as the Green New Deal.

Those longstanding efforts come as the DSA gains political ground following the 2026 midterm elections, with DSA-backed candidates winning congressional, state legislative and local school board races.

DSA-backed positions prevailed on 23 ballot measures, including a measure in Washington where voters rejected a proposal to restore expanded parental rights in public schools.

The election results highlight the growing political reach of an organization whose members advocate shifting economic power away from private corporations and expanding government-funded programs, including universal healthcare and childcare.

The 2025 victory of New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani, a card-carrying member of the DSA, heightened awareness of the movement's growing influence, particularly among young people. Following the 2026 midterms, three card-carrying DSA members — Rep. Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez, D-N.Y., and Reps.-elect Claire Valdez and Darializa Avila Chevalier, both New York Democrats — are set to represent parts of New York City in Congress.

Marré said the American Parents Coalition is working to equip parents with resources to identify and question political activism in schools, including through its "Lookout" initiative.

She warned that the impact could become more visible as newly elected DSA-backed officials take office.

"We're gonna see a lot more of the firsthand resources and the effects of this brand new class of DSA members being folded into their communities," Marré said.

Elaine Mallon is a writer for Fox News Digital and Fox Business covering national politics.

VisionQuest Is Great, But Disney's Similar Sci-Fi Fantasy Series From 5 Years Ago Is Still Superior

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

As expected, VisionQuest is a creative, clever, and intriguing new entry in the Marvel Cinematic Universe, though it still can't quite live up to its genre-bending predecessor. Starring returning MCU favorites Paul Bettany and James Spader as Vision and Ultron, respectively, VisionQuest concludes a superhero journey that began in the much-maligned yet no less impactful Avengers: Age of Ultron, examining what it means to feel, love, and be human. Bettany and Spader are both excellent, and the show is especially appealing to die-hard MCU fans, a substantial reward for a decade of franchise loyalty.

Visually, many of VisionQuest's scenes are stunning, especially when Ultron and fellow familiar MCU AIs like F.R.I.D.A.Y., J.A.R.V.I.S., DUM-E, U, and Spider-Man: Far From Home's E.D.I.T.H. spend their time rooting around in Vision's Sherlockian, 1960s-inspired Mind Palace, digging deep into his hard drive to help this new and not-quite-improved version of Vision understand and accept his identity. He has the original Vision's memories, but only because Wanda Maximoff recreated him in her hex. What does that make White Vision? And how does that impact his relationship with Tommy Maximoff, Wanda and Westview-Vision's conjured son? It's a lot to wrap one's mind around.

While there's so much to like, and the show has set up an exciting finale, VisionQuest also suffers, at times, from a lack of focus. It ping-pongs between Vision's increasingly chaotic mind, his father-son bonding time with Tommy, Paladin's contract, Ultron's shady desires, and a new synthezoid's homicidal killing spree. It forgets what made Marvel's first Disney+ show so effective. Despite all the magical sitcom hijinks and SWORD's desperation to break into Westview, WandaVision's story always revolved around one specific, relatable, emotional theme.

Even now, five years and well over a dozen shows later, WandaVision remains one of the MCU's most impressive TV achievements, a surprising but highly effective blend of psychological, character-driven storytelling and a fantastical, mysterious superhero plot. Though WandaVision also had multiple storylines, including flashbacks to Wanda's life after Avengers: Endgame and SWORD's operation to break the Westview Hex, everything referenced or directly impacted Wanda's overwhelming grief and her hopeless desire to uphold her newly imagined life.

Vision, Tommy, Billy, Agatha Harkness, and even Monica Rambeau were all supporting players in her story. It never lost its focus. The same isn't true for VisionQuest. Ultron's, Tommy's, and Jocasta's needs and wants are all just as important as Vision's in the story's overall narrative arc. Add characters like Phineas Horton and JIM (both played brilliantly by Bettany), Tommy's friend Lisa, and multiple personified AIs, and it feels more like an ensemble drama than a character study.

As the penultimate episode of the series, VisionQuest episode 7 features a very exciting collection of Easter eggs, references, and key MCU ties.

There's nothing inherently wrong with that, of course — WandaVision's first "unofficial" sequel, Agatha All Along, was also an ensemble show, though that choice felt more purposeful. Agatha needed those characters to achieve her goals, and since their story was always bound together, the series never lost sight of its base narrative. Does Vision's journey to accept his existence and his innate ability to feel need all those moving parts? It's hard to say.

Both WandaVision and Agatha All Along also had a more cohesive creative "vision," as it were. Wanda's fascination with sitcoms served an undeniable purpose, as did the genre-mixing Witches' Road trials in Agatha All Along. They provided a structural framework. Between Vision's mental mansion, Jocasta's white void, London, and Madripoor, VisionQuest sometimes feels like watching multiple shows simultaneously, playing into well-worn superhero tropes. VisionQuest's version of the Hex and the Witches' Road is the digital mansion, but the story too often pivots in a new direction.

That said, VisionQuest is still a genuinely compelling story, a worthy end to the Marvel Cinematic Universe's unofficial Scarlet Witch trilogy. It's certainly leagues better than some of the MCU's other rushed shows, including Secret Invasion and She-Hulk: Attorney at Law. Like WandaVision and Agatha All Along, it explores universal themes like love, grief, identity, and parenthood with emotional finesse, all while experimenting with new kinds of storytelling. If this is where Vision's story ends — Bettany has not (yet) been confirmed for Avengers: Doomsday — then it's an undeniably effective and honorable sendoff.

All three series showcase how inventive superhero stories can be. Marvel doesn't need to be boxed in. It can explore different genres and structures, telling smaller, character-driven stories while still pushing the wider franchise forward. Like one of the MCU's other best trilogies, James Gunn's Guardians of the Galaxy, this is the story of the Maximoff family: a family made real and held together by love and heartache. There's nothing more human than that. The best superhero stories make these otherworldly, overpowered beings feel relatable and flawed. That's exactly what VisionQuest and the rest of Marvel's Scarlet Witch trilogy do too.

VisionQuest's finale releases Tuesday, November 10 on Disney+.

Scientists simulate an Earth without life to help hunt for aliens and inhabited worlds

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Sign up to our monthly entertainment newsletter to keep up with all our coverage of the latest sci-fi and space movies, tv shows, games and books.

Space.com's Sci-Fi Reader's Club. Read a sci-fi short story every month and join a virtual community of fellow science fiction fans!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Life can leave its mark on a planetary atmosphere, so learning what properties are the product of non-life will help astronomers identify those worlds that might have life.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

A new computer simulation that depicts Earth, or a rocky planet in general, without life is the latest tool in the quest to discover whether or not we are alone in the universe.

Life leaves its mark on a planet, particularly its atmosphere, in the form of a mixture of biochemically created gases. Some are obvious, while others can be produced by both biology and geology, meaning that distinguishing a planet that has life from one that does not, solely based on what gases we can detect in its atmosphere, can be difficult. The key is in knowing which atmospheric conditions can be explained without life.

To aid in distinguishing between habitable and inhabited planets, planetary scientist Samantha Gilbert-Janizek of the University of California, Riverside, developed a model that simulates the evolution of a rocky planet without life.

"The model can help us see how habitable but non-living planets might mimic living planets," Gilbert-Janizek, who was a PhD student at the University of Washington when the research was conducted, said in a statement.

Gilbert-Janizek built upon a model previously developed by Washington’s Rory Barnes that simulated the core, mantle, and crust of a planet. This new model pushed the simulation two steps further by incorporating the surface and atmosphere of the planet as well.

In all, the model simulates 19 properties of a rocky planet that are not related to life, including the amount of heat a planet receives from its star, the exchange of heat and chemicals from the core to the surface, and the outflow of certain gases from the subsurface.

Gilbert-Janizek’s starting point was to simulate the development of Earth had life never arisen here, therefore providing a baseline with which to compare to exoplanets. She ran the simulation from the birth of Earth 4.5 billion years ago to 500 million years into the future. The result was a lifeless world, but one that still had oceans and an atmosphere.

"When we interpret observations of an exoplanet’s atmosphere, this tool will help us determine the probability that what we observe indicates life on the surface," said Gilbert-Janizek.

What makes the simulation especially useful is its plug-and-play nature. Researchers can simply apply the bulk properties of whichever planet they are studying to the model, and then just let it run. If the result is a planet with an atmosphere that matches what is detected spectroscopically by the James Webb Space Telescope or, in the future, the various extremely large 30-meter-plus ground-based telescopes or NASA’s planned Habitable Worlds Observatory, then we know that planet probably does not have life as we know it.

However, if the simulated planet without life does not match what we observe on the real planet, then it becomes possible that life is present and altering the atmosphere.

"Dr Gilbert-Janizek’s work represents an exciting step toward understanding where life can exist in the universe,” said Barnes. "After more than a decade of work by many scientists, Dr Gilbert-Janizek has finally completed a model of the Earth that simultaneously reproduces properties of Earth’s core, mantle, surface and atmosphere. That calibration gives us confidence that future simulations of exoplanets will be accurate."

While any deviation from what is predicted by the model will not necessarily mean there is definitely life on a planet, it will help pick out those worlds that are worthy of follow-up studies by the next generation of telescopes.

Gilbert-Janizek’s model is described in a paper published in The Planetary Science Journal.

Keith Cooper is a freelance science journalist and editor in the United Kingdom, and has a degree in physics and astrophysics from the University of Manchester. He's the author of "The Contact Paradox: Challenging Our Assumptions in the Search for Extraterrestrial Intelligence" (Bloomsbury Sigma, 2020) and has written articles on astronomy, space, physics and astrobiology for a multitude of magazines and websites.

The Argentina and Inter Milan great worked with some of the game’s most influential coaches

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

During his 22-year playing career, Javier Zanetti turned out more than 1,000 times for club and country.

The Argentina and Inter Milan great shared a dressing room with some of the greatest players of his generation and played under some of the game’s most influential coaches.

Few, however, left quite the same impression as Marcelo Bielsa, who handed Zanetti 52 international caps during his six-year spell in charge of Argentina.

Bielsa’s reputation for demanding the highest level of commitment from his players is well-documented, but for Zanetti, the former Leeds United manager’s eccentric methods went way beyond mere tactics and training sessions.

"He is a manager who knows how to get the absolute best out of his players,” Zanetti tells FourFourTwo. That was true for me, and it has been true for so many others.”

For Zanetti, what sets Bielsa apart from other elite coaches is his ability to connect on a much deeper, more meaningful level.

“He has a way of reaching something deep inside you, of reigniting that pure love of the game you had as a kid,” he continues.

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

“It was a wonderful six years working with him with the national team."

During that time, Zanetti’s international career began to flourish, with the defender able to benefit from playing under Bielsa for a sustained period.

A year after Bielsa left the Argentina job, a new era was ushered in when Lionel Messi made his debut in a 2005 friendly against Hungary.

Over the next seven years, Zanetti and Messi would share a pitch for Argentina 37 times, giving the Inter Milan star the opportunity to get a front-row seat for the accession of one of the game’s all-time greats.

“I was lucky enough to play alongside him for six or seven years with Argentina, right at his peak, and when he was just breaking into the national team,” Zanetti adds.

“He could, and still can, produce something that nobody sees coming. Quite simply, the greatest player who ever lived.”

In addition to his journalist work, Mewis is also the author of four football history books that include times on Leeds United and the England national team. Now working as a digital marketing coordinator at Harrogate Town, too, Mewis counts some of his best career moments as being in the iconic Spygate press conference under Marcelo Bielsa and seeing his beloved Leeds lift the Championship trophy during lockdown.

Shop like a pro this Black Friday: How to spend your money wisely and get a great deal

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

My inbox is flooded with Black Friday deals, but it’s hard to know what’s actually worth buying and what will drop to the same price in the new year. And while we all want a bargain, are these sales ever as good as they seem?

I've been working as a home writer and product tester long enough to know that some discounts aren’t always as good as they seem, and I have devised my own method for deciding what’s worth picking up on sale and what’s best left behind.

These are my 3 top tips for shopping wisely this Black Friday.

It might be called the Black Friday sales, but you really don’t have to wait until after Thanksgiving to get a bargain. Retailers don’t wait for the short window to sell discounted products; they maximize their sales throughout the whole of November. You’ll find plenty of products are discounted before the holidays, which we’ve already been covering at Tom’s Guide.

Right now, for instance, I spotted an excellent 60% price cut on Chefman’s Indoor Pizza Oven, discounted to $199 at Amazon. While my colleague and expert barista, Erin Bashford, has shared a great $200 price drop on the Breville Barista Express, which is $499 at Amazon.

If you don’t stay ahead of the game, popular products and colorways may already be sold out by the time Black Friday arrives. And why wait until after Thanksgiving to enjoy a product that could give you some serious clout when you're hosting?

Do a bit of snooping before you press ‘checkout’ to see if the discount is genuine or if the price was super-inflated right before the sale. You can use free tools like CamelCamelCamel to track Amazon price histories.

I used the price checker on one of my favorite KitchenAid stand mixers — KitchenAid’s Artisan 5-quart tilt-head stand mixer in the milkshake colorway. It’s currently listed at $349 at Amazon, down from $499.

But is the 30% discount as good as it looks? The price checker says yes: it is the lowest price in the past 12 months, beating its $400 average, and undercutting Walmart’s current $399 price tag. If I didn’t already have a KitchenAid stand mixer on my countertop, it would be straight in my basket.

You can also check Google Shopping to find the best price. Right now, it shows the same stand mixer for $349 at Williams Sonama. However, it won’t give you the extensive historical data that CamelCamelCamel provides, since it only shows 30 days of prices.

As a dedicated home baker and interior enthusiast, Black Friday isn’t about buying novelty gadgets — it’s the time of year I hunt for investments. I look for appliances and decor items that are usually outside my budget. It’s the ultimate excuse to upgrade my home and my kitchen without paying full price.

This is part of my strategy, and I’m rarely swayed by a product just because it’s on sale. I take a pragmatic approach and treat the sales as a targeted event rather than an impulsive shopping spree.

I keep a running wish list of appliances and homeware throughout the year, and I try my best to enforce a ‘cool-down’ period before hitting the checkout. It gives me a reality check on whether that niche gadget I desperately needed last month still feels like an essential today. If the initial excitement has worn off, it comes off the list.

What does this look like in reality? New kitchen knives that are easy to sharpen, like Ninja’s Premium Knives, are staying on my wish list; I consider them a kitchen essential for meal prep. But I’ll think more about whether I really need yet another comfy throw to snuggle up on my sofa. Perhaps Ugg’s Duffield Throw, reduced to $83 at Amazon, might end up in Santa's sleigh, if I’m good!

Ninja's 12-piece knife set has a built-in sharpener, so you never have to use a dull knife again. It includes an 8-inch chef knife, a 5-inch santoku knife, a 5-inch utility knife, and a 3.5-inch paring knife, plus 6 steak knives with a 4.5 inch blade. A pair of shears completes the set.

NASA's destroyed Swift Observatory took one of the best-ever photos of bright Andromeda
